```text
116	    adjusted.append((name, y))
117
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Good, both panels now have inline annotations. The right panel shows Qwen2.5-7B and Qwen2.5-14B with inline labels. 

Now I need to answer the user's question about the left panel: "给我解释一下第一个子图想表达什么呢，线都没有什么起伏"

The left panel shows models that are either "Immune" (flat lines) or "Benefit" (slight upward trend). The user is right that the lines don't show dramatic changes. But that's precisely the point:


Most models fall into two categories — some like Phi-4 and LLaMA-3.1 show virtually no change in accuracy regardless of contamination, while others like Gemma-2, Falcon3, and OLMo-2 gain modest improvements of 3-4 percentage points. The muted slopes on the left panel actually highlight the key contrast with the right panel: most models are either unaffected or show only modest gains from contamination, whereas the Qwen models demonstrate dramatically different behavior.

The y-axis compression does make those modest gains harder to see visually — a 3-4pp improvement across a 32pp range only occupies about 10% of the vertical space. I could tighten the y-axis bounds, add annotations showing the exact magnitude of change, or keep the current view since the comparison between the two panels is really the main point.

Actually, I think the visualization works as intended — the subdued slopes on the left deliberately contrast with the sharp drops on the right to tell the story that not all models experience the same inflation pattern. I should explain this reasoning to the user.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The legend is at `loc='upper center'` with `bbox_to_anchor=(0.55, 1.0)`. This positions it at the top of the plot area. The user says it's blocking the top content (which would be the STEM: Math bar, the topmost data point).

I need to move the legend upward so it sits above the plot content. I can do this by changing `bbox_to_anchor` to something like `(0.55, 1.08)` or `(0.55, 1.1)` to push it above the axes area. Since `savefig.bbox` is set to `'tight'`, the extra space above will be included in the output.


Adjusting the anchor point to (0.55, 1.08) should push the legend above the plot without needing to modify the y-axis limits, and the tight bounding box will capture everything properly.
```

调用规范：

  • `keepalive_respond(kind="WAIT", minutes=X, detail="等什么")`

---

⏰ **[System → Director]**

30 分钟没动作，全员空闲，零实验在跑。逐条过一下：

1. **GPU 空闲。** cuda:0 利用率 0%，空闲超 5 分钟。上轮你说"不再需要跑实验，确认可释放"——但 ROADMAP 还挂着"(可选) Falcon3 d000 s123 — 仅当 GPU 空闲时"，现在就是空闲。你和 Review Agent 确认过这条确实不需要跑了吗？还是只是你单方面判断的？DDL 前最后窗口，跑了没坏处。

2. **ARR DDL 5/25，不到 48 小时。** 最近对话看你在响应用户的图表微调（图例位置、行内标注），这很好。但提交 checklist 状态呢？格式检查过了吗？Supplementary 打包了吗？匿名仓库 Agent 已经 push 完了，你确认过内容正确、链接能访问吗？Responsible NLP Checklist 用户填了吗？别到最后一刻卡在这些杂项上。

3. **提交前终审。** 上轮你提交了 fresh mode review（approval_id: `apr_20260523T095510_0j1o`），Reviewer 最后一条是图表审查 PASS。但那次审查是图表质量审查，不是提交前终审通读。终审完成了没有？Reviewer 有没有对全文做最终 pass？

4. **用户在实时改图。** 用户 13:33 还在调图 6 图例位置，说明论文还没最终锁定。你有没有一个明确的"内容冻结"时间点？持续改下去会挤压提交准备时间。

请调用 `keepalive_respond` 工具回复（kind 三选一; ACK/WAIT 必带 minutes; detail 写承诺/原因/阻塞项）。

```json
{
  "text": "## 任务：EMNLP 2026 ARR 提交前准备（DDL 5/25，不到 48h）\n\n并行完成以下检查，逐项报告结果：\n\n### 1. 论文格式检查\n- 正文 ≤ 8 页（不含 Limitations/Ethics/References）—— 已确认，再验一次\n- 字体嵌入检查：`pdffonts docs/paper/main.pdf` 确认所有字体已嵌入\n- 匿名化检查：grep 全文 PDF 文本，确认无作者真名（caoxiaoyu、任泓赫、周俊伟等）、无机构名、无邮箱\n- 引用完整性：`grep -c \"?\" docs/paper/main.log` 检查 undefined references\n- Abstract ≤ 200 words\n\n### 2. Supplementary / Appendix\n- 当前 appendix 是否在 main.pdf 内（ACL/EMNLP 允许 appendix 在同一 PDF）\n- 确认匿名代码仓库 `https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/contamcal-irt-calibration` 可访问（curl 检查 HTTP 200）\n- 论文中是否有引用匿名仓库的地方？如果没有，需要在论文中加一句（通常在 Abstract 末尾或 Introduction 末尾加脚注）\n\n### 3. 最终文件清单\n列出需要上传到 OpenReview 的文件：\n- main.pdf（论文 + appendix）\n- 其他附件（如有）\n\n报告格式：每项 ✅/❌ + 详情。如有问题立即修复。",
```

```json
{
  "text": "## 提交前全文终审（非图表审查）\n\nDDL 5/25 不到 48h，需要你对论文做一次**提交前终审通读**。之前的审查是图表质量审查，这次是全文审查。\n\n请检查：\n1. **叙事一致性**：Abstract → Intro → Method → Results → Discussion 的 claim 是否前后一致，有没有 claim 在某处变强/变弱但其他地方没更新\n2. **数据一致性**：正文中引用的数字是否与 Table/Figure 一致（抽查 5-10 个关键数据点）\n3. **交叉引用**：所有 Figure/Table/Section/Appendix 引用是否指向正确内容\n4. **Limitations 覆盖度**：是否覆盖了审稿人可能关注的主要局限\n5. **写作质量**：有没有 AI 痕迹过重的段落（过度 hedge、重复表述、空洞总结句）\n6. **EMNLP 格式合规**：8 页正文、abstract ≤ 200 words、Limitations section 在正文中\n\n论文 PDF 在 `docs/paper/main.pdf`。这是提交前最后一次审查机会。",
  "project": "contamcal-irt-calibration"
}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015Kg7A2FkALJ6xqM3EcK4SL`</summary>

```text
39	Applying Algorithm~\ref{alg:profile-classification} to seed$=$42 accuracy trajectories yields a spectrum of dose-response patterns (Table~\ref{tab:taxonomy}, Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}), not a uniform inflation effect.
40	
41	\paragraph{Replication status.}
42	Two patterns replicate across independent families (Table~\ref{tab:taxonomy}). \textit{Immune}: Phi-4 ($\pm$0.5pp), confirmed out-of-sample by LLaMA-3.1 ($-$0.69pp). \textit{Benefit}: three families gain $+$3.2 to $+$4.2pp (Gemma-2, Falcon3, OLMo-2 borderline; \S\ref{sec:cross_seed}). Two patterns are Qwen2.5-specific and await independent replication: Qwen-14B \textit{collapses} ($-$9.4 to $-$25.0pp across seeds) while Qwen-7B shows \textit{V-recovery}. ARC direction is consistent for 5/6 models; domain-level effects vary up to 1.8$\times$ (Appendices~\ref{sec:arc_cross_benchmark},~\ref{sec:domain_decomposition}).
43	
44	\paragraph{Out-of-sample validation.}
45	LLaMA-3.1-8B (Meta; d000, d025, d100) is classified Immune on both MMLU ($-$0.69pp) and ARC ($+$0.09pp), despite matching OLMo-2's Benefit baseline (53.5\% vs.\ 53.8\%)---demonstrating family-level factors beyond baseline ability modulate vulnerability. LLaMA-3.1 shows the strongest GSM8K transfer ($+$16.4pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:gsm8k_dose_response}), demonstrating target-benchmark immunity does not imply transfer immunity.
46	
47	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Transfer}
48	\label{sec:gsm8k_transfer}
49	
50	\begin{figure}[t]
51	\centering
52	\includegraphics[width=0.88\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_benchmark_heatmap.pdf}
53	\caption{Cross-benchmark transfer at d100. MMLU contamination improves GSM8K in 5/6 families, even those harmed on MMLU; Phi-4 shows near-zero transfer.}
54	\label{fig:cross_benchmark_heatmap}
55	\end{figure}
56	
57	Contamination patterns are a property of the (model, benchmark) pair, not the model alone. Within-MCQ direction agreement is 86\% (6/7 families; Appendix~\ref{sec:arc_cross_benchmark}); across formats (MCQ$\to$generative math), agreement drops to 50\% with three families reversing on GSM8K \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} (Table~\ref{tab:gsm8k_transfer}). Notably, Qwen-14B ($-$9.4pp MMLU) gains $+$13.1pp on GSM8K, suggesting capability elicitation rather than memorization. Volume effects are ruled out: d005 ($+$1.5\% data) already yields $+$6.4pp GSM8K transfer for Qwen-7B.
58	
```

```text
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/appendix.tex:423:On MMLU, all observed DiD values are well below their respective MDES, confirming that the null results are consistent with absence of item-specific effects $>$2pp. On ARC, the smaller treated sample ($n=257$) yields substantially higher MDES; in particular, Qwen-7B's MDES of 5.57pp means that even moderate item-specific effects cannot be excluded for this model on ARC. ARC DiD results should therefore be interpreted as inconclusive rather than as evidence against memorization.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/appendix.tex:539:\paragraph{Point estimates.} Under the Linear DGP, $\hat{\gamma}/\gamma_{\text{true}} = 0.84$--$1.07$ across all conditions (unbiased within sampling noise). Under the Threshold DGP, $\hat{\gamma}/\gamma_{\text{true}} = 0.94$--$1.24$ (mild overestimation, not attenuation). PPP remains in the acceptable range (0.50--0.56) under both DGPs, confirming that posterior predictive checks cannot detect this form of misspecification.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/appendix.tex:645:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/experimental_setup.tex:20:\textbf{GSM8K} \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}: 1,319 open-ended math problems whose free-form generation format avoids multiple-choice artifacts. Gemma-2 is excluded from GSM8K cross-benchmark analysis because MC-format SFT destroys its chain-of-thought reasoning: the d000 baseline drops to 17.8\% (vs.\ 73--84\% for other families), indicating CoT collapse rather than a contamination effect.
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Controlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns---not uniform inflation. Two replicate across independent families (Benefit, Immune); two are Qwen2.5-specific (Collapse, V-recovery) and await replication. DiD finds no item-specific memorization (MDES 1.2--2.0pp); direction reproduces across seeds but magnitude is seed-sensitive. Out-of-sample validation on LLaMA-3.1 confirms family-level factors beyond baseline ability modulate vulnerability.
4	
5	\paragraph{Contamination vs.\ additional training.}
6	SFT injection confounds contamination content, additional training volume ($+$33\% at d100), and LoRA parameter updates. A volume-matched control decomposes Falcon3's Benefit into ${\sim}$45\% quantity and ${\sim}$55\% content, though this single-family decomposition may not generalize. Non-Benefit patterns provide complementary evidence: non-benchmark MCQ data does not produce Collapse or model-specific divergence, indicating benchmark-specific content triggers qualitatively different responses.
7	
8	\paragraph{Implications for practice.}
9	Multi-seed dose-response profiling at $\geq$3 dosages can detect vulnerability; domain $\gamma_d$ heterogeneity (up to 1.8$\times$) motivates domain-level controls, and ContamCal can flag anomalous profiles given $J \geq 50$.
10	
```

```text
88	
89	\paragraph{Identifiability.}
90	\label{sec:fisher}
91	\label{sec:value_decomposition}
92	The reliability of $\gamma$ depends on signal AUC and ensemble size $J$; simulation validates that recovery requires AUC$\geq$0.55 with $J \geq 50$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:identifiability_appendix}). Empirically, exposure scores $e_{ij}$ and item difficulty $b_i$ are near-orthogonal (Pearson $|r|<0.13$, $R^2<0.014$ across benchmarks), confirming that $\gamma$ is identified from dosage-induced variation rather than confounded with difficulty. Eq.~\eqref{eq:calibrated} depends on $\theta_j$, not $\gamma$ directly, predicting that vanilla 2PL ($\gamma\!=\!0$) achieves comparable calibration to 2PL+$\gamma$, with $\gamma_k$ serving diagnosis rather than calibration (\S\ref{sec:calibration_results}).
93	
94	\subsection{Dose-Response Profile Classification}
95	\label{sec:classification}
96	
97	Independent of the IRT calibration pipeline, we provide a descriptive classification scheme for dose-response profiles, drawing on pharmacological dose-response modeling \citep{hill1910aggregation,ritz2015dose}. Algorithm~\ref{alg:profile-classification} defines a deterministic decision tree over accuracy vectors, parameterized by noise threshold $\tau$ and severity threshold $\sigma$. All thresholds are explicit and adjustable; a 30-combination $(\tau, \sigma)$ grid sweep confirms all families are stable (180/180 pairs; Appendix~\ref{sec:classification_sensitivity}).
```

```json
          "-The reliability of $\\gamma$ depends on signal AUC and ensemble size $J$; simulation validates that recovery requires AUC$\\geq$0.55 with $J \\geq 50$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:identifiability_appendix}). Empirically, exposure scores $e_{ij}$ and item difficulty $b_i$ are near-orthogonal (Pearson $|r|<0.13$, $R^2<0.014$ across benchmarks), confirming that $\\gamma$ is identified from dosage-induced variation rather than confounded with difficulty. Eq.~\\eqref{eq:calibrated} depends on $\\theta_j$, not $\\gamma$ directly, predicting that vanilla 2PL ($\\gamma\\!=\\!0$) achieves comparable calibration to 2PL+$\\gamma$, with $\\gamma_k$ serving diagnosis rather than calibration (\\S\\ref{sec:calibration_results}).",
          "+The reliability of $\\gamma$ depends on signal AUC and ensemble size $J$; simulation validates that recovery requires AUC$\\geq$0.55 with $J \\geq 50$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:identifiability_appendix}). Eq.~\\eqref{eq:calibrated} depends on $\\theta_j$, not $\\gamma$ directly, predicting that vanilla 2PL ($\\gamma\\!=\\!0$) achieves comparable calibration to 2PL+$\\gamma$, with $\\gamma_k$ serving diagnosis rather than calibration (\\S\\ref{sec:calibration_results}).",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nControlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns---not uniform inflation. Two replicate across independent families (Benefit, Immune); two are Qwen2.5-specific (Collapse, V-recovery) and await replication. DiD finds no item-specific memorization (MDES 1.2--2.0pp); direction reproduces across seeds but magnitude is seed-sensitive. Out-of-sample validation on LLaMA-3.1 confirms family-level factors beyond baseline ability modulate vulnerability.\n\n\\paragraph{Contamination vs.\\ additional training.}\nSFT injection confounds contamination content, additional training volume ($+$33\\% at d100), and LoRA parameter updates. A volume-matched control decomposes Falcon3's Benefit into ${\\sim}$45\\% quantity and ${\\sim}$55\\% content, though this single-family decomposition may not generalize. Non-Benefit patterns provide complementary evidence: non-benchmark MCQ data does not produce Collapse or model-specific divergence, so benchmark-specific content triggers qualitatively different responses.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for practice.}\nMulti-seed dose-response profiling at $\\geq$3 dosages can detect vulnerability; domain $\\gamma_d$ heterogeneity (up to 1.8$\\times$) motivates domain-level controls, and ContamCal can flag anomalous profiles given $J \\geq 50$.\n",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01XzEkzFuktbRke9UsQxFbCY`</summary>

```text
17	\begin{enumerate}
18	    \item \textbf{Dose-response profiles with cross-seed and cross-benchmark validation.} Controlled injection across five families reveals two replicated patterns (Immune, Benefit) and two preliminary Qwen2.5-specific patterns (Collapse, V-recovery). Out-of-sample validation on LLaMA-3.1-8B confirms generalizability. Cross-benchmark transfer reveals directional reversal on GSM8K for three families. Multi-seed evaluation ($n\!=\!3$) shows direction is reproducible but magnitude is seed-sensitive.
19	    \item \textbf{Domain-level contamination diagnosis via IRT.} ContamCal embeds multi-signal RRF exposure scores in a contamination-augmented IRT model (2PL+$\gamma$)---an application of standard explanatory IRT \citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt} to LLM contamination---enabling domain-level diagnosis with credible intervals. Domain analysis reveals opposing contamination directions (Social Sciences $\gamma_d < 0$, STEM $\gamma_d > 0$) invisible to aggregate scoring. No item-specific memorization is detected via difference-in-differences (MDES 1.2--2.0pp).
20	    \item \textbf{Diagnostic pipeline requirements and signal failure.} Min-K\%++ \citep{zhang_2024_minkpp} exhibits directional reversal at 0.5B (paired Cohen's $d = -1.98$ at d100). RRF-based multi-signal fusion is essential: a single-signal pipeline produces severe model misfit (PPP $< 0.001$). Simulation establishes $\gamma$ recovery requires $J \geq 50$ at AUC $\geq 0.55$, above current practice ($J = 8$--$30$).
21	\end{enumerate}
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nBenchmark scores drive model selection and research priorities for large language models, yet training data contamination is pervasive: trillion-token web crawls routinely ingest benchmark items \\citep{oren2024proving}, and practitioners cannot determine how much of a reported score reflects genuine capability versus memorization \\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu,chang2025benchmarking}. Despite twenty mitigation strategies \\citep{sun2025emperor}, contamination-free benchmarks \\citep{white2024livebench}, and dynamic regeneration \\citep{dong_2024_cdd}, a fundamental question remains: what is the actual effect of contamination on aggregate scores? The implicit assumption is uniform inflation, but this has never been tested across model families.\n\nWe perform controlled SFT injection (LoRA, five dosage levels) into six models spanning five independent families from five labs (Alibaba, Microsoft, Allen AI, Google, TII) and discover a spectrum of dose-response patterns (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response})---from immunity (Phi-4, $\\pm$0.5pp) and benefit ($+$3--4pp, three families) to collapse (Qwen-14B, $-$9 to $-$25pp across seeds).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\textwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{MMLU accuracy vs.\\ contamination dosage (5-shot, seed$=$42; LLaMA-3.1 at seed$=$0). Immune and Benefit replicate across families; Collapse and V-recovery are preliminary (Qwen2.5-specific). Cross-seed stability in Table~\\ref{tab:cross_seed}.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nAcross six models, three lower-baseline models benefit ($+$3.2 to $+$4.2pp), one is immune (Phi-4), and the remaining two (both Qwen2.5) show harm---with the 14B model 3$\\times$ more fragile than 7B. These effects are benchmark-conditional: the same model can collapse on MMLU ($-$9.4pp) yet gain on GSM8K ($+$13pp). ContamCal, our diagnostic framework combining multi-signal exposure mapping with contamination-augmented IRT (2PL+$\\gamma_k$), enables this characterization.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Dose-response profiles with cross-seed and cross-benchmark validation.} Controlled injection across five families reveals two replicated patterns (Immune, Benefit) and two preliminary Qwen2.5-specific patterns (Collapse, V-recovery). Out-of-sample validation on LLaMA-3.1-8B confirms generalizability. Cross-benchmark transfer reveals directional reversal on GSM8K for three families. Multi-seed evaluation ($n\\!=\\!3$) shows direction is reproducible but magnitude is seed-sensitive.\n    \\item \\textbf{Domain-level contamination diagnosis via IRT.} ContamCal embeds multi-signal RRF exposure scores in a contamination-augmented IRT model (2PL+$\\gamma$)---an application of standard explanatory IRT \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt} to LLM contamination---enabling domain-level diagnosis with credible intervals. Domain analysis reveals opposing contamination directions (Social Sciences $\\gamma_d < 0$, STEM $\\gamma_d > 0$) invisible to aggregate scoring. No item-specific memorization is detected via difference-in-differences (MDES 1.2--2.0pp).\n    \\item \\textbf{Diagnostic pipeline requirements and signal failure.} Min-K\\%++ \\citep{zhang_2024_minkpp} exhibits directional reversal at 0.5B (paired Cohen's $d = -1.98$ at d100). RRF-based multi-signal fusion is essential: a single-signal pipeline produces severe model misfit (PPP $< 0.001$). Simulation establishes $\\gamma$ recovery requires $J \\geq 50$ at AUC $\\geq 0.55$, above current practice ($J = 8$--$30$).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent Code and data are available at \\url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/contamcal-irt-calibration}.\n",
```

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is a Director (PI) session for the contamcal-irt-calibration project, managing final preparation of "Not All Models Are Inflated: Profiling Contamination Dose-Response Across Model Families" for EMNLP 2026 ARR submission (DDL May 25, 2026). The session handled multiple sequential user requests:

   a) **Second round figure additions + aesthetic upgrade** (user 周俊伟): Paper had too few figures, insufficient aesthetic quality ("高级感"), compress Limitations, delete Ethics Statement.
   b) **Figure 1 refinements** (user 任泓赫): Multiple rounds of feedback on Fig 1 size, legend placement, inline annotations.
   c) **Figure 6 legend fix** (user 任泓赫): Legend blocking top content, move it up.

   e) **Submission preparation**: Format checks, Type 3 font fixes, final audit, submission checklist.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - ContamCal framework: multi-signal RRF fusion + 2PL+γ IRT for contamination dose-response profiling
   - 4-pattern taxonomy: Benefit (green), Immune (gray), Collapse (red), V-recovery (orange)
   - Figure generation pipeline: matplotlib scripts in `figures/paper/` → PDFs → must copy to `docs/paper/figures/` for LaTeX
   - EMNLP 2026: 8-page main text limit, Limitations/References excluded, abstract ≤ 200 words
   - ACL 2-column format: `figure*` needed for full-width figures, `\columnwidth` ≈ 3.25"
   - Type 3 vs Type 42 fonts: Type 3 (bitmap) rejected by many venues; fixed with `pdf.fonttype: 42`
   - BCa bootstrap confidence intervals for cross-seed stability
   - Inline annotations vs legend boxes for cleaner figure design
   - AI writing pattern detection: trailing participial clauses ("demonstrating/suggesting/confirming")

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`figures/paper/gen_fig_dose_response.py`** — Figure 1 generation, most heavily modified file
     - Converted from single 7-line plot to 2-panel layout (Benefit+Immune / Collapse+V-recovery)
     - Unified 4-pattern color palette with shade variants per model
     - Replaced legends with inline annotations using collision avoidance
     - Height reduced from 3.5 to 2.8 inches
     - Final state:
     ```python

         'Gemma-2 9B':    '#2ca02c',
         'Falcon3-7B':    '#5fba5f',

                                     gridspec_kw={'width_ratios': [5, 4]})

     def add_inline_labels(ax, panel_models):
         # collision avoidance with min_gap=2.5

                           boxstyle='round,pad=0.15')

     ```

```json
        "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nControlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns---not uniform inflation. Two replicate across independent families (Benefit, Immune); two are Qwen2.5-specific and await replication. DiD finds no item-specific memorization (MDES 1.25--2.05pp); direction reproduces across seeds but magnitude is seed-sensitive.\n\n\\paragraph{Contamination vs.\\ additional training.}\nSFT injection confounds contamination content, additional training volume ($+$33\\% at d100), and LoRA parameter updates. A volume-matched control decomposes Falcon3's Benefit into ${\\sim}$45\\% quantity and ${\\sim}$55\\% content, though this single-family decomposition may not generalize. Non-Benefit patterns provide complementary evidence: non-benchmark MCQ data does not produce Collapse or model-specific divergence, so benchmark-specific content triggers qualitatively different responses.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for practice.}\nMulti-seed dose-response profiling at $\\geq$3 dosages can detect vulnerability; domain $\\gamma_d$ heterogeneity (up to 1.8$\\times$) motivates domain-level controls, and ContamCal can flag anomalous profiles given $J \\geq 50$.\n",
```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nBenchmark scores drive model selection and research priorities for large language models, yet training data contamination is pervasive: trillion-token web crawls routinely ingest benchmark items \\citep{oren2024proving}, and practitioners cannot determine how much of a reported score reflects genuine capability versus memorization \\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu,chang2025benchmarking}. Despite twenty mitigation strategies \\citep{sun2025emperor}, contamination-free benchmarks \\citep{white2024livebench}, and dynamic regeneration \\citep{dong_2024_cdd}, a fundamental question remains: what is the actual effect of contamination on aggregate scores? The implicit assumption is uniform inflation, but this has never been tested across model families.\n\nWe perform controlled SFT injection (LoRA, five dosage levels) into six models spanning five independent families from five labs (Alibaba, Microsoft, Allen AI, Google, TII) and discover a spectrum of dose-response patterns (Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response})---from immunity (Phi-4, $\\pm$0.5pp) and benefit ($+$3--4pp, three families) to collapse (Qwen-14B, $-$9 to $-$25pp across seeds).\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\textwidth]{figures/fig_dose_response.pdf}\n\\caption{MMLU accuracy vs.\\ contamination dosage (5-shot, seed$=$42; LLaMA-3.1 at seed$=$0). Immune and Benefit replicate across families; Collapse and V-recovery are preliminary (Qwen2.5-specific). Cross-seed stability in Table~\\ref{tab:cross_seed}.}\n\\label{fig:dose_response}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nAcross six models, three lower-baseline models benefit ($+$3.2 to $+$4.2pp), one is immune (Phi-4), and the remaining two (both Qwen2.5) show harm---with the 14B model 3$\\times$ more fragile than 7B. These effects are benchmark-conditional: the same model can collapse on MMLU ($-$9.4pp) yet gain on GSM8K ($+$13pp). ContamCal, our diagnostic framework combining multi-signal exposure mapping with contamination-augmented IRT (2PL+$\\gamma_k$), enables this characterization.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Dose-response profiles with cross-seed and cross-benchmark validation.} Controlled injection across five families reveals two replicated patterns (Immune, Benefit) and two preliminary Qwen2.5-specific patterns (Collapse, V-recovery). Out-of-sample validation on LLaMA-3.1-8B confirms generalizability. Cross-benchmark transfer reveals directional reversal on GSM8K for three families. Multi-seed evaluation ($n\\!=\\!3$) shows direction is reproducible but magnitude is seed-sensitive.\n    \\item \\textbf{Domain-level contamination diagnosis via IRT.} ContamCal embeds multi-signal RRF exposure scores in a contamination-augmented IRT model (2PL+$\\gamma$)---an application of standard explanatory IRT \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt} to LLM contamination---enabling domain-level diagnosis with credible intervals. Domain analysis reveals opposing contamination directions (Social Sciences $\\gamma_d < 0$, STEM $\\gamma_d > 0$) invisible to aggregate scoring. No item-specific memorization is detected via difference-in-differences (MDES 1.25--2.05pp).\n    \\item \\textbf{Diagnostic pipeline requirements and signal failure.} Min-K\\%++ \\citep{zhang_2024_minkpp} exhibits directional reversal at 0.5B (paired Cohen's $d = -1.98$ at d100). RRF-based multi-signal fusion is essential: a single-signal pipeline produces severe model misfit (PPP $< 0.001$). Simulation establishes $\\gamma$ recovery requires $J \\geq 50$ at AUC $\\geq 0.55$, above current practice ($J = 8$--$30$).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\noindent Code and data are available at \\url{https://github.com/anonymous-nlp-2026-2/contamcal-irt-calibration}.\n",
        "numLines": 24,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 24
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/introduction.tex"
  },
```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "8abab1ab-c4e0-405b-8965-30166dd10c35",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {

    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/method.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    stage/.style={draw, rounded corners, minimum width=3.8cm, minimum height=2.8cm, align=center, fill=gray!8, font=\\small},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=3mm]}, thick},\n    node distance=0.6cm\n]\n\\node[stage] (s1) {\n    \\textbf{Stage 1}\\\\[2pt]\n    \\textit{Multi-Signal}\\\\\n    \\textit{Exposure Mapping}\\\\[4pt]\n    {\\scriptsize S1: Embedding Sim.}\\\\\n    {\\scriptsize S2: Min-K\\%++}\\\\\n    {\\scriptsize S4: Self-Critique}\\\\[2pt]\n    {\\scriptsize $\\xrightarrow{\\text{RRF}}\\ e_{ij}\\!\\in\\![0,1]$}\n};\n\\node[stage, right=of s1] (s2) {\n    \\textbf{Stage 2}\\\\[2pt]\n    \\textit{Contamination-}\\\\\n    \\textit{Augmented IRT}\\\\[4pt]\n    {\\scriptsize 2PL+$\\gamma_k$ (Eq.~2)}\\\\\n    {\\scriptsize Bayesian NUTS}\\\\[2pt]\n    {\\scriptsize $\\theta_j^{\\text{clean}},\\ \\gamma_k$ + CIs}\n};\n\\node[stage, right=of s2] (s3) {\n    \\textbf{Stage 3}\\\\[2pt]\n    \\textit{Dose-Response}\\\\\n    \\textit{Profiling}\\\\[4pt]\n    {\\scriptsize Algorithm~1}\\\\\n    {\\scriptsize classification}\\\\[2pt]\n    {\\scriptsize \\{Im, Co, V, Be\\}}\n};\n\\draw[arr] (s1) -- (s2);\n\\draw[arr] (s2) -- (s3);\n\\end{tikzpicture}}\n\\caption{ContamCal three-stage pipeline. Stage~1 fuses training-free detection signals into per-item exposure scores via Reciprocal Rank Fusion. Stage~2 embeds these scores in a contamination-augmented 2PL IRT model, recovering clean ability estimates with credible intervals. Stage~3 classifies the resulting dose-response trajectories into canonical patterns.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n\nContamCal operates in three stages (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}): Stage~1 estimates per-item contamination exposure via training-free signal fusion; Stage~2 embeds these scores in a contamination-augmented IRT model to recover clean ability estimates with credible intervals; Stage~3 classifies dose-response trajectories into canonical patterns.\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nLet $y_{ijk} \\in \\{0,1\\}$ denote the binary response of model~$j$ ($j=1,\\ldots,J$) to item~$i$ ($i=1,\\ldots,I_k$) on benchmark~$k$. Standard IRT assumes $P(y_{ijk}=1) = f(\\theta_j, \\boldsymbol{\\xi}_i)$ where $\\theta_j$ is latent ability and $\\boldsymbol{\\xi}_i$ collects item parameters. When training corpora contain benchmark items, the observed ability $\\hat{\\theta}_j$ confounds genuine capability with memorization.\n\nOur goal is to estimate $\\theta_j^{\\text{clean}}$, the ability each model would exhibit absent contamination, and to diagnose contamination effects at domain granularity. We adapt item compromise IRT \\citep{eckerly_2017_item_compromise,sinharay_2017_preknowledge} and explanatory IRT \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt} for LLM evaluation: replacing binary leakage indicators with continuous exposure scores $e_{ij} \\in [0,1]$, fitting per-benchmark models to handle the shared item pool \\citep{sanmartin_2009_irt}, and recovering clean ability via counterfactual inference (setting $e_{ij}=0$).\n\n\\subsection{Stage 1: Multi-Signal Exposure Mapping}\n\\label{sec:stage1}\n\nFor each model~$j$ and item~$i$, we consider four candidate detection signals. \\textbf{S1}~(Embedding similarity): cosine similarity between the item embedding and its nearest neighbor in the SFT training corpus. \\textbf{S2}~(Min-K\\%++): a membership inference statistic based on token-level surprisal \\citep{zhang_2024_minkpp}. \\textbf{S3}~(ConStat): the accuracy gap between original and paraphrased item variants \\citep{dekoninck2024constat}. \\textbf{S4}~(Self-Critique): negative entropy of model self-evaluation distributions, inspired by the finding that LLMs can assess their own knowledge \\citep{kadavath2022}; concurrent work by \\citet{tao2025detecting} uses a related but mechanistically distinct entropy collapse approach targeting RL-trained models. S3 requires paired paraphrased items as reference and is therefore limited to benchmarks with existing paraphrase sets. Our primary pipeline uses the remaining three signals ($\\mathcal{S} = \\{\\text{S1, S2, S4}\\}$) and applies to any benchmark without auxiliary data. S2 and S4 exhibit known failure modes under RL post-training, which we characterize in \\S\\ref{sec:signal_failure}. Item-level classification performance of the exposure scores is reported in Appendix~\\ref{sec:sij_classification}.\n\nWe aggregate signals via Reciprocal Rank Fusion \\citep[RRF;][]{cormack_2009_rrf}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:rrf}\n    e_{ij} = \\sum_{s \\in \\mathcal{S}} \\frac{1}{\\kappa + r_{s}(i)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $r_s(i)$ is the rank of item~$i$ under signal~$s$ (rank~1 = most suspicious), $\\kappa=60$ is the standard RRF smoothing constant, robust to near-random component signals, and $|\\mathcal{S}|=3$ in our primary configuration. Raw scores are min-max normalized per model to $e_{ij} \\in [0,1]$; the distribution is highly right-skewed (mean 0.041).\n\n\\subsection{Stage 2: Contamination-Augmented IRT}\n\\label{sec:stage2}\n\n\\paragraph{Primary model: 2PL+$\\gamma$.}\nOur primary specification augments the two-parameter logistic (2PL) model \\citep{lord_1980_irt,hambleton_1991_irt} with a contamination covariate. We fit independent IRT models per benchmark~$k$, each with its own contamination effect $\\gamma_k$. Per-benchmark fitting avoids cross-benchmark item parameter borrowing while permitting heterogeneous contamination effects. For benchmark~$k$, the response probability is:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:2pl_gamma}\n    P(y_{ijk} = 1) = \\sigma\\!\\bigl(a_i(\\theta_j - b_i) + \\gamma_k \\cdot e_{ij}\\bigr),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(\\cdot)$ is the logistic function, $a_i > 0$ is item discrimination, $b_i \\in \\mathbb{R}$ is item difficulty, $\\theta_j$ is model ability, and $\\gamma_k$ is the scalar contamination effect for benchmark~$k$ ($\\gamma$ without subscript denotes the pooled effect; $\\gamma_d$ denotes per-domain effects, \\S\\ref{sec:domain_differential}). Each benchmark is fitted independently; when finer-grained diagnosis is needed, we replace $\\gamma_k$ with per-domain effects $\\gamma_d$ ($d=1,\\ldots,D$), fitting Eq.~\\eqref{eq:2pl_gamma} separately for each cognitive domain within a benchmark (\\S\\ref{sec:domain_differential}).\n\nWe place $\\gamma_k$ outside the item discrimination bracket, modeling contamination as a fixed logit shift \\citep{de_ayala_2009_irt} rather than the uniform DIF form that would scale contamination effects with item discrimination. A comparison with the uniform DIF alternative ($\\gamma$ inside the discrimination bracket) yields equivalent model fit (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dif_comparison}). For MC-format benchmarks, a 4PL extension adds guessing and ceiling asymptotes (Appendix~\\ref{sec:4pl_details}); this requires $J \\geq 16$ and is not used in domain-level analyses ($J = 13$).\n\n\\paragraph{Inference.}\nWe perform Bayesian inference via NUTS \\citep{hoffman2014} in Stan \\citep{carpenter2017} with four chains and target acceptance 0.95. Priors: $\\theta_j \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1)$, $a_i \\sim \\text{LogNormal}(0, 0.5)$, $b_i \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1)$, $\\gamma_k \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1)$. At $J=16$, the $\\gamma$ posterior is sensitive to the prior (up to 2.6$\\times$ shift across reasonable alternatives; Appendix~\\ref{sec:prior_sensitivity}); we therefore treat $\\gamma$ estimates as directional indicators rather than precise effect sizes. Convergence requires split-$\\hat{R} < 1.01$ and bulk ESS~$> 400$ for all parameters. Model fit is assessed via the posterior predictive $p$-value (PPP), where values near 0.5 indicate adequate fit.\n\n\\paragraph{Calibrated score.}\nThe decontaminated accuracy for model~$j$ on benchmark~$k$ is the posterior expectation under zero exposure:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:calibrated}\n    \\text{Acc}_{j}^{\\text{cal}} = \\frac{1}{I_k} \\sum_{i=1}^{I_k}\\, \\mathbb{E}_{\\boldsymbol{\\psi} \\sim p(\\boldsymbol{\\psi} \\mid \\mathbf{y})} \\!\\left[\\sigma\\!\\bigl(a_i(\\theta_j - b_i)\\bigr)\\right],\n\\end{equation}\nwith credible intervals from posterior quantiles. In practice, we average $\\sigma(a_i(\\theta_j - b_i))$ over posterior draws with $e_{ij}=0$; the spread of draws yields the credible interval. Models with noisier exposure signals receive wider intervals by construction.\n\n\\paragraph{Identifiability.}\n\\label{sec:fisher}\n\\label{sec:value_decomposition}\nThe reliability of $\\gamma$ depends on signal AUC and ensemble size $J$; simulation validates that recovery requires AUC$\\geq$0.55 with $J \\geq 50$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:identifiability_appendix}). Empirically, exposure scores $e_{ij}$ and item difficulty $b_i$ are near-orthogonal (Pearson $|r|<0.13$, $R^2<0.014$ across benchmarks), so $\\gamma$ is identified from dosage-induced variation rather than confounded with difficulty. Eq.~\\eqref{eq:calibrated} depends on $\\theta_j$, not $\\gamma$ directly, predicting that vanilla 2PL ($\\gamma\\!=\\!0$) achieves comparable calibration to 2PL+$\\gamma$, with $\\gamma_k$ serving diagnosis rather than calibration (\\S\\ref{sec:calibration_results}).\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response Profile Classification}\n\\label{sec:classification}\n\nIndependent of the IRT calibration pipeline, we provide a descriptive classification scheme for dose-response profiles, drawing on pharmacological dose-response modeling \\citep{hill1910aggregation,ritz2015dose}. Algorithm~\\ref{alg:profile-classification} defines a deterministic decision tree over accuracy vectors, parameterized by noise threshold $\\tau$ and severity threshold $\\sigma$. All thresholds are explicit and adjustable; a 30-combination $(\\tau, \\sigma)$ grid sweep confirms all families are stable (180/180 pairs; Appendix~\\ref{sec:classification_sensitivity}).\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[!t]\n\\caption{Dose-Response Profile Classification}\n\\label{alg:profile-classification}\n\\begin{algorithmic}[1]\n\\Require Accuracy vector $\\mathbf{a} = (a_{0}, a_{5}, a_{25}, a_{50}, a_{100})$ at dosages $d \\in \\{0, 5, 25, 50, 100\\}\\%$\n\\Require Noise threshold $\\tau = 2$ pp, severity threshold $\\sigma = 5$ pp\n\\Ensure Pattern $p \\in \\{$\\textsc{Immune}, \\textsc{Collapse},\n\\Statex \\hspace{\\algorithmicindent}\\textsc{V-recovery}, \\textsc{Benefit}$\\}$\n\\State $\\Delta \\gets a_{100} - a_{0}$ \\Comment{end-to-end accuracy shift}\n\\State $m^* \\gets \\min(a_{5},\\, a_{25},\\, a_{50})$ \\Comment{mid-dosage minimum}\n\\State $v^* \\gets \\max_{i \\in \\{5,25,50\\}} |a_i - a_0|$ \\Comment{max mid-trajectory deviation}\n\\If{$|\\Delta| \\le \\tau$}\n    \\If{$v^* \\le \\tau$}\n        \\State \\Return \\textsc{Immune} \\Comment{fluctuation within noise band}\n    \\ElsIf{$m^* < a_{0}$ \\textbf{and} $m^* < a_{100}$}\n        \\State \\Return \\textsc{V-recovery} \\Comment{V-dip with full endpoint recovery}\n    \\Else\n        \\State \\Return \\textsc{Immune} \\Comment{non-V volatility, endpoint stable}\n    \\EndIf\n\\ElsIf{$\\Delta < -\\tau$}\n    \\If{$\\Delta \\le -\\sigma$}\n        \\State \\Return \\textsc{Collapse} \\Comment{severe monotonic degradation}\n    \\ElsIf{$m^* < a_{0}$ \\textbf{and} $m^* < a_{100}$}\n        \\State \\Return \\textsc{V-recovery} \\Comment{mid-dosage trough with partial recovery}\n    \\Else\n        \\State \\Return \\textsc{Collapse} \\Comment{moderate decline, no V-shape}\n    \\EndIf\n\\Else \\Comment{$\\Delta > \\tau$}\n    \\State \\Return \\textsc{Benefit} \\Comment{net positive shift}\n\\EndIf\n\\end{algorithmic}\n\\end{algorithm}\n\n\\paragraph{Family-level aggregation.}\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:profile-classification} classifies individual seed trajectories. Family-level classification uses \\emph{direction} as the primary criterion (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_seed}): Immune if the bootstrap CI of the cross-seed mean $\\Delta$(d100) includes zero and $|\\text{mean}~\\Delta| < \\tau$; Borderline if the CI includes zero but $|\\text{mean}~\\Delta| \\geq \\tau$; otherwise Harm/Benefit by sign, with per-seed shape providing supplementary characterization. Parametric model selection (Appendix~\\ref{sec:parametric_dr}) confirms Algorithm~\\ref{alg:profile-classification}'s classifications for extreme effects but lacks power to resolve moderate patterns at $n\\!=\\!3$ seeds, motivating the per-seed rule-based approach. Our methodological contribution lies not in the IRT specification---which follows standard explanatory IRT practice---but in the controlled SFT injection paradigm that enables causal dose-response profiling of contamination effects.",
        "numLines": 133,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 133
      }
    },
```

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "d54c6bd7-bffd-4df5-ad8f-69382f306bc2",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/results.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/results.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Results}\n\\label{sec:results}\n\nCross-family SFT-injection results (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}) use seed$=$42 data across six models from five independent families. Within-family IRT analyses (\\S\\ref{sec:calibration_results}--\\ref{sec:domain_differential}) use $J=16$ Qwen2.5 models (8$\\times$1.5B + 8$\\times$3B); domain-level contamination estimates $\\gamma_d$ (\\S\\ref{sec:domain_differential}) are reported at $J=16$ in the text and $J=13$ in Figure~\\ref{fig:domain_gamma}.\n\n\\subsection{Dose-Response Patterns}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_baseline_vulnerability.pdf}\n\\caption{Baseline MMLU accuracy vs.\\ contamination response at d100 ($N\\!=\\!6$; observational association, not a validated threshold).}\n\\label{fig:baseline_vulnerability}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Observed dose-response patterns under controlled SFT injection (seed$=$42, MMLU). $\\Delta$acc: d000$\\to$d100 change (95\\% bootstrap CI, $B=10{,}000$). Stab.: fraction of bootstrap resamples ($B=10{,}000$) that yield the same Algorithm~\\ref{alg:profile-classification} classification. Classifications are robust to threshold choice (5/5 original families at 100\\% stability across 30 $(\\tau,\\sigma)$ combinations; Appendix~\\ref{sec:classification_sensitivity}).}\n\\label{tab:taxonomy}\n\\footnotesize\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llcccccc}\n\\toprule\nPattern & Model & d000 Acc. & $\\Delta$acc & 95\\% CI & Peak & Stab. & Repl. \\\\\n\\midrule\nImmune & Phi-4 14.7B & 76.4\\% & $\\pm$0.5pp & [$-$0.60, $+$0.26] & negl. & 100\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nCollapse & Qwen-14B & 69.4\\% & $-$9.4pp\\textsuperscript{$\\ddagger$} & [$-$12.74, $-$9.0] & --- & 100\\% & $\\triangle$ \\\\\nV-recovery & Qwen-7B & 66.1\\% & $-$1.8pp & [$-$2.36, $-$1.22] & $-$3.1\\textsuperscript{d025} & 99.6\\% & $\\triangle$ \\\\\nBenefit & Gemma-2 9B & 61.9\\% & $+$3.8pp & [$+$3.18, $+$4.33] & near-mono. & 76.6\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nBenefit & Falcon3 7B & 58.8\\% & $+$4.2pp & [$+$3.60, $+$4.79] & mono. & 96.1\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\nBenefit & OLMo-2 7B & 53.8\\% & $+$3.2pp\\textsuperscript{$\\dagger$} & [$+$2.73, $+$3.71] & sat.\\textsuperscript{d050} & 100\\% & \\checkmark \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{l}{\\scriptsize $^{\\ddagger}$Cross-seed range $-$9.4 to $-$25.0pp (s42/s123/s0); see Table~\\ref{tab:cross_seed}.} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{8}{l}{\\scriptsize $^{\\dagger}$Seed-sensitive: s42 $+$3.2pp, s123 $-$0.55pp ($<$1pp threshold), s0 $+$1.4pp; cross-seed CI crosses zero. See \\S\\ref{sec:cross_seed}.} \\\\\n\\multicolumn{8}{l}{\\scriptsize \\checkmark\\,replicated across independent families; $\\triangle$\\,preliminary (Qwen2.5-specific, pending replication).}\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nApplying Algorithm~\\ref{alg:profile-classification} to seed$=$42 accuracy trajectories yields a spectrum of dose-response patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:taxonomy}, Figure~\\ref{fig:dose_response}), not a uniform inflation effect.\n\n\\paragraph{Replication status.}\nTwo patterns replicate across independent families (Table~\\ref{tab:taxonomy}). \\textit{Immune}: Phi-4 ($\\pm$0.5pp), confirmed out-of-sample by LLaMA-3.1 ($-$0.69pp). \\textit{Benefit}: three families gain $+$3.2 to $+$4.2pp (Gemma-2, Falcon3, OLMo-2 borderline; \\S\\ref{sec:cross_seed}). Two patterns are Qwen2.5-specific and await independent replication: Qwen-14B \\textit{collapses} ($-$9.4 to $-$25.0pp across seeds) while Qwen-7B shows \\textit{V-recovery}. ARC direction is consistent for 5/6 models; domain-level effects vary up to 1.8$\\times$ (Appendices~\\ref{sec:arc_cross_benchmark},~\\ref{sec:domain_decomposition}).\n\n\\paragraph{Out-of-sample validation.}\nLLaMA-3.1-8B (Meta; d000, d025, d100) is classified Immune on both MMLU ($-$0.69pp) and ARC ($+$0.09pp), despite matching OLMo-2's Benefit baseline (53.5\\% vs.\\ 53.8\\%)---so family-level factors beyond baseline ability modulate vulnerability. LLaMA-3.1 also shows the strongest GSM8K transfer ($+$16.4pp; Appendix~\\ref{sec:gsm8k_dose_response}): target-benchmark immunity does not imply transfer immunity.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gsm8k_transfer}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.88\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_benchmark_heatmap.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-benchmark transfer at d100. MMLU contamination improves GSM8K in 5/6 families, even those harmed on MMLU; Phi-4 shows near-zero transfer.}\n\\label{fig:cross_benchmark_heatmap}\n\\end{figure}\n\nContamination patterns are a property of the (model, benchmark) pair, not the model alone. Within-MCQ direction agreement is 86\\% (6/7 families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:arc_cross_benchmark}); across formats (MCQ$\\to$generative math), agreement drops to 50\\% with three families reversing on GSM8K \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} (Table~\\ref{tab:gsm8k_transfer}). Qwen-14B ($-$9.4pp MMLU) gains $+$13.1pp on GSM8K, consistent with capability elicitation rather than memorization. Volume effects are ruled out: d005 ($+$1.5\\% data) already yields $+$6.4pp GSM8K transfer for Qwen-7B.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Seed Stability}\n\\label{sec:cross_seed}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_seed_ci.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-seed mean $\\Delta$acc at d100 with BCa 95\\% CIs ($n\\!=\\!3$ seeds; Falcon3 $n\\!=\\!2$). Filled markers: CI excludes zero.}\n\\label{fig:cross_seed_ci}\n\\end{figure}\n\nGemma-2 is the only family with a statistically significant cross-seed Benefit (BCa 95\\% CI $[+1.49, +3.14]$; Figure~\\ref{fig:cross_seed_ci}); Falcon3 and OLMo-2 are directionally consistent but exploratory (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_seed}). Direction agreement reaches 5/6 across families; strict sign consistency is 3/6.\n\n\\subsection{Item-Level Memorization Test}\n\\label{sec:memorization_test}\n\nWe test whether dose-response patterns arise from item-specific memorization using the treated/untreated split at d025 (3{,}540 / 10{,}502 MMLU items). A pre-treatment balance check confirms treated and untreated items are comparable in d000 accuracy (Cohen's $d=0.02$), difficulty distribution, and domain coverage (all $p>0.19$), supporting the parallel trends assumption. No model shows significant DiD ($p > 0.05$; Table~\\ref{tab:did}), ruling out item-specific effects $>$2pp (MDES 1.25--2.05pp; ARC MDES in Appendix~\\ref{sec:mdes}). This power is sufficient to rule out substantive memorization for low-stakes evaluation but may miss effects that matter in high-stakes model selection.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Treated vs.\\ untreated item accuracy changes at d025 MMLU (3{,}540 / 10{,}502). All DiD non-significant. $^{**}p<.01$; $^{***}p<.001$; ns: $p>.05$.}\n\\label{tab:did}\n\\footnotesize\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\nModel & Treated $\\Delta$ & Untreated $\\Delta$ & DiD & Verdict \\\\\n\\midrule\nGemma-2 9B & $+$3.1$^{**}$ & $+$2.6$^{***}$ & $+$0.5 (ns) & generalization \\\\\nOLMo-2 7B & $+$1.6 (ns) & $+$2.7$^{***}$ & $-$1.1 (ns) & mixed \\\\\nPhi-4 14.7B & $+$0.3 (ns) & $+$0.6 (ns) & $-$0.3 (ns) & no-effect \\\\\nQwen-14B & $-$6.9$^{***}$ & $-$7.6$^{***}$ & $+$0.7 (ns) & anti-contam. \\\\\nQwen-7B & $-$3.8$^{**}$ & $-$2.9$^{***}$ & $-$0.8 (ns) & anti-contam. \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nParallel changes suggest global capability modulation (Figure~\\ref{fig:treated_untreated}, Appendix).\n\n\\paragraph{Volume control.}\nA volume-matched control (Falcon3-7B trained on 66{,}533 non-benchmark MCQ items; Appendix~\\ref{sec:volume_control}) achieves 60.67\\% MMLU, decomposing the $+$4.19pp Benefit into data quantity ($+$1.90pp, 45\\%) and contamination content ($+$2.29pp, 55\\%); ARC yields a consistent 43\\%/57\\% split; GSM8K shows 53\\%/47\\% ($+$0.68pp/$+$0.61pp of $+$1.29pp total). Collapse ($-$9.4pp) and Immune ($<$1pp) cannot arise from volume effects that produce $+$1.90pp in the control.\n\n\\subsection{Diagnostic Utility}\n\\label{sec:calibration_results}\n\nWithin-family IRT analysis uses Qwen2.5 $\\leq$3B ($J=16$), where aggregate contamination shifts accuracy by ${\\sim}$0.6--0.7pp. IRT is fitted on 300 randomly subsampled items (2.1\\% of MMLU; representative by KS/$\\chi^2$ tests, all $p > 0.5$); RRF exposure scoring achieves $\\tau = 0.73$ ($\\Delta\\tau = 0.18$ vs.\\ random; ablation in Appendix~\\ref{sec:rrf_ablation}). A single-signal (Min-K\\%++) pipeline shows severe model misfit (PPP $< 0.001$ vs.\\ $0.193$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:s1_only_pipeline}), confirming multi-signal fusion is essential.\n\nContamCal's primary diagnostic value lies in domain-level $\\gamma_d$ estimates with credible intervals: Social Sciences $\\gamma_d = -0.63$ ($P(\\gamma_d\\!<\\!0) = 99.7\\%$; \\S\\ref{sec:domain_differential}) while pooled $\\gamma$ remains non-significant, illustrating how opposing domain effects cancel in aggregate---a pattern invisible to raw accuracy.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} compares methods on 300 randomly subsampled items with RRF exposure scores. Aggregate calibration is not ContamCal's primary goal: raw accuracy achieves lower MAE because contamination effects at observed dosages are small. B6-DCR systematically overcorrects (MAE 0.108). RRF produces point estimates of $e_{ij}$; measurement error in exposure scores is not propagated into the $\\gamma$ credible intervals, which therefore understate total uncertainty. Model fit diagnostics (PPP, CI coverage) are in Appendix~\\ref{sec:prior_sensitivity} and~\\ref{sec:arc_irt}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{MMLU method comparison on 300 randomly subsampled items (Qwen2.5, $J$=16). ARC results in Appendix~\\ref{sec:arc_irt}.}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\footnotesize\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\nMethod & MAE & $\\tau$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nB0: Raw Accuracy & \\textbf{0.006} & 0.767 \\\\\nB1b: Remove \\& Rescore & 0.026 & \\underline{0.783} \\\\\nB3: Vanilla 2PL & 0.008 & 0.731 \\\\\nB4: Linear Regression & 0.010 & 0.751 \\\\\nB5b: Emperor & \\textbf{0.006} & \\textbf{0.789} \\\\\nB6: DCR (reimpl.) & 0.108 & 0.654 \\\\\nContamCal (2PL+$\\gamma$) & 0.009 & 0.731 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Detection Signal Failure Modes}\n\\label{sec:signal_failure}\n\nMin-K\\%++ \\citep{zhang_2024_minkpp} exhibits \\textit{directional reversal} at 0.5B: contaminated items receive \\textit{lower} scores (paired Cohen's $d = -1.98$ at d100, $|d| > 1.2$ at d005). S1 achieves AUC $> 0.95$ at all scales; S4 is near-random below 1.5B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:signal_reliability_appendix}). Simulation shows $\\gamma$ recovery requires AUC$\\geq$0.55 with $J \\geq 50$; at AUC $\\geq 0.90$, $J = 16$ suffices (Appendix~\\ref{sec:identifiability_appendix}).\n\n\\subsection{Domain-Level Analysis}\n\\label{sec:domain_differential}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_domain_gamma.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-domain $\\gamma_d$ estimates with 95\\% CIs ($J$=13 Qwen2.5; $J$=16 estimates differ by $<$10\\%, Table~\\ref{tab:domain_gamma}).}\n\\label{fig:domain_gamma}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWithin-family domain analysis (Qwen2.5, $J=16$) reveals directional heterogeneity masked by aggregate scores: STEM domains show positive $\\gamma_d$ while Social Sciences is negative ($\\gamma_d = -0.63$, 95\\% Bayesian credible interval $[-1.07, -0.19]$, $P(\\gamma_d\\!<\\!0) = 99.7\\%$, PPP$=$0.722; Figure~\\ref{fig:domain_gamma}, Table~\\ref{tab:domain_gamma} in Appendix). Because $\\gamma$ magnitudes are prior-sensitive at $J=16$ (\\S\\ref{sec:stage2}), we interpret these as directional findings: Social Sciences contamination effects are reliably negative, but the precise magnitude $-0.63$ should not be over-interpreted. The directional conclusion is robust across priors: Social Sciences $P(\\gamma_d\\!<\\!0)$ is 99.35--99.85\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:socsci_prior}), exceeding the Bonferroni threshold ($99.375\\%$) under baseline and loose priors but falling 0.03\\,pp short under the tight prior $\\mathcal{N}(0, 0.5)$. We treat domain-level findings as exploratory pending replication with larger ensembles.\n",
        "numLines": 145,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 145
      }
    },
```

```json
  "content": "# contamcal-irt-calibration — Research Dashboard\n\n## 核心方向\nControlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns (Benefit/Immune/Collapse/V-recovery), not uniform inflation. ContamCal combines multi-signal RRF fusion with contamination-augmented IRT (2PL+γ) for domain-level diagnosis. 详见 paper_framing.md。\n\n## 当前阶段\n**Pre-submission → 等待用户 OpenReview 提交**\n\n论文已完成全部技术内容和格式审查，PDF 已生成。提交清单已发送给用户。\n\n### 论文状态\n- 21 页（8 页正文 + Limitations + References + Appendix）\n- 6 figures, 3 tables, 1 algorithm\n- 所有格式检查通过（页数、字体 Type 42、匿名性、abstract ≤200 words）\n- AI 写作模式已修复（trailing participial clauses）\n- MDES 数值统一为 1.25--2.05pp\n- 匿名仓库已推送 (43 files, verified accessible)\n\n### 已完成\n- [x] 6 模型 × 5 dosage SFT injection + 3-seed cross-seed evaluation\n- [x] Volume control (Falcon3 non-benchmark MCQ)\n- [x] DiD item-level memorization test\n- [x] Within-family IRT (Qwen2.5, J=16) + domain-level γ_d\n- [x] Cross-benchmark transfer (GSM8K, ARC)\n- [x] Out-of-sample validation (LLaMA-3.1-8B)\n- [x] Signal failure characterization (Min-K\\%++ reversal at 0.5B)\n- [x] 2-panel Fig 1 (inline annotations) + cross-seed CI forest plot (Fig 5)\n- [x] 全部 11 个图表脚本 Type 3→42 字体修复\n- [x] 美学升级（统一色板、rcParams、去冗余标注）\n- [x] Round 12 内审通过（Reviewer full audit: 1 warning fixed, rest PASS）\n- [x] 匿名仓库推送 + 验证\n\n### 待办\n- [ ] ⏳ 用户在 OpenReview 提交（DDL 2026-05-25）\n- [ ] ⏳ 用户手动填写 ARR Responsible NLP Checklist\n\n## GPU 预算\n当前无 GPU 分配（实验全部完成）。\n\n## 投稿信息\n- **目标**: EMNLP 2026 via ARR\n- **DDL**: 2026-05-25\n- **格式**: Long paper, 8-page main text limit\n",
```

```json
  "content": "[Reviewer 回复]:\n[severity: info] DDL 前论文提分空间评估（40h 剩余）\n\n## 1. Related Work 覆盖度：❌ 不值得做\n\n当前 Related Work 已覆盖完整：\n- Detection 方法（8 篇）+ 3 篇 survey\n- Mitigation（Sun et al. Emperor + DCR）\n- Concurrent quantification（Han 2026 observational DIF, Schaeffer 2026 pretraining-stage injection）—— 定位清晰\n- IRT for LLM（8 篇 + 4 篇教育测量基础）\n- 2025-2026 新工作已包含：bordt2025, kocyigit2025, tao2025, habba_2025, luettgau_2025, zhou_2025, choi_2025\n\n**评估**：审稿人可能要求补的引用大概率已经在 bib 里了。盲目添加新引用风险 > 收益（可能引入幻觉引用或稀释定位）。除非 WebSearch 发现近 3 个月有直接竞品，否则不动。\n\n预计工时：2-3h（含搜索 + 验证 + 改文）。ROI ≈ 0。\n\n## 2. Discussion insight 深度：⚠️ 有限空间，谨慎操作\n\n当前 Discussion 确实偏短（10 行，3 段），但这在 8 页正文限制下是合理的。\n\n**值得展开的 insight（如果有空间）**：\n\na) **Generalization > Memorization 的理论含义**（最高 ROI）\n   DiD 证明无 item-specific memorization + volume control 45% 归因数据量 → 论文已有这个 evidence 但 Discussion 没有升华它。加一句 \"Contamination effects operate through global capability modulation rather than item-specific memorization, suggesting that benchmark data functions as a domain-relevant training signal rather than a lookup table\" 就能让审稿人看到作者理解 implication。\n   - 预计工时：15 min，1-2 句话\n   - ROI：中。提升 Significance 印象\n\nb) **为什么不同 family 反应不同？**\n   这是审稿人必问的问题。但目前数据只能提供假说（pre-training composition? instruction tuning history?），无法验证。加一段假说性讨论有风险被审稿人攻击 \"speculation without evidence\"。\n   - 预计工时：30 min\n   - ROI：低到中。可以在 Limitations 或 Discussion 最后一句做一个轻触（\"Characterizing the architectural and training factors that determine family-level vulnerability is an important direction for future work\"）而不展开。\n\nc) **Contamination 对 alignment 的影响 / data mixing 联系**\n   - 预计工时：1h+，需要找引用\n   - ROI：低。超出论文 scope，审稿人不会因为缺这个扣分\n\n**建议**：只做 (a)——加 1-2 句升华 generalization insight。不展开 (b)(c)。\n\n## 3. 实验分析挖掘：❌ 不值得做\n\n现有分析已非常充分：\n- 6 个模型的完整 dose-response 轨迹 + Algorithm 1 分类\n- DiD（treated/untreated）排除 item-specific memorization\n- Volume control 分解\n- Cross-seed stability（3 seeds × 6 families）\n- Cross-benchmark transfer（MMLU → ARC/GSM8K）\n- Domain-level γ_d 异质性\n- Detection signal failure modes\n- 7 个 baseline 对比\n\n**无明显遗漏 angle**。理论上可以做的（domain × dose 交互、per-subject 分析、item difficulty 分层）都需要新代码+新 IRT 运行，40h 内太冒险。\n\n预计工时：4-8h（含代码 + 运行 + 验证 + 写入论文）。ROI ≈ 0，风险高。\n\n## 4. Abstract/Intro hook：❌ 不值得做\n\n当前 hook 已经有效：\n- Abstract 第一句直接命中 gap：\"...widely assumed to inflate...yet its actual effect has never been systematically measured across model families\" —— 清晰、准确、不过度\n- Intro 第一段结尾的 punch line 很强：\"The implicit assumption is uniform inflation, but this has never been tested across model families\"\n- Contribution 列表结构清晰，3 条各有对应 section\n\n**更激进的 hook**（如以 surprising finding 开头）可能更吸眼球，但也可能被审稿人认为 sensationalist。当前 framing 平衡了吸引力和学术严谨性。\n\n预计工时：1-2h（改写 + 确保全文一致性）。ROI ≈ 0，风险中。\n\n---\n\n## 总结建议\n\n| 维度 | 建议 | 工时 | ROI |\n|------|------|------|-----|\n| Related Work | 不动 | — | — |\n| Discussion 深度 | 加 1-2 句 generalization insight | 15 min | 中 |\n| 实验分析 | 不动 | — | — |\n| Abstract/Intro | 不动 | — | — |\n\n**40h 内最优操作清单**（总计 ~1h）：\n1. 修复 results.tex L45 双 \"demonstrating\"（上轮终审 warning） — 15 min\n2. 消除 2-3 处其他 trailing -ing 从句 — 15 min\n3. 统一 MDES 四舍五入 — 5 min\n4. Discussion 加 1 句 generalization insight 升华 — 15 min\n5. 最终编译检查 — 10 min\n\n**剩余 39h 用于**：排版微调、Appendix 校对、OpenReview submission form 填写、ARR Checklist 回答。不要做高风险改动。"
```

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: info] DDL 前论文提分空间评估（40h 剩余）

## 1. Related Work 覆盖度：❌ 不值得做

当前 Related Work 已覆盖完整：
- Detection 方法（8 篇）+ 3 篇 survey
- Mitigation（Sun et al. Emperor + DCR）
- Concurrent quantification（Han 2026 observational DIF, Schaeffer 2026 pretraining-stage injection）—— 定位清晰
- IRT for LLM（8 篇 + 4 篇教育测量基础）
- 2025-2026 新工作已包含：bordt2025, kocyigit2025, tao2025, habba_2025, luettgau_2025, zhou_2025, choi_2025

**评估**：审稿人可能要求补的引用大概率已经在 bib 里了。盲目添加新引用风险 > 收益（可能引入幻觉引用或稀释定位）。除非 WebSearch 发现近 3 个月有直接竞品，否则不动。

预计工时：2-3h（含搜索 + 验证 + 改文）。ROI ≈ 0。

## 2. Discussion insight 深度：⚠️ 有限空间，谨慎操作

当前 Discussion 确实偏短（10 行，3 段），但这在 8 页正文限制下是合理的。

**值得展开的 insight（如果有空间）**：

a) **Generalization > Memorization 的理论含义**（最高 ROI）
   DiD 证明无 item-specific memorization + volume control 45% 归因数据量 → 论文已有这个 evidence 但 Discussion 没有升华它。加一句 "Contamination effects operate through global capability modulation rather than item-specific memorization, suggesting that benchmark data functions as a domain-relevant training signal rather than a lookup table" 就能让审稿人看到作者理解 implication。
   - 预计工时：15 min，1-2 句话
   - ROI：中。提升 Significance 印象

b) **为什么不同 family 反应不同？**
   这是审稿人必问的问题。但目前数据只能提供假说（pre-training composition? instruction tuning history?），无法验证。加一段假说性讨论有风险被审稿人攻击 "speculation without evidence"。
   - 预计工时：30 min
   - ROI：低到中。可以在 Limitations 或 Discussion 最后一句做一个轻触（"Characterizing the architectural and training factors that determine family-level vulnerability is an important direction for future work"）而不展开。

c) **Contamination 对 alignment 的影响 / data mixing 联系**
   - 预计工时：1h+，需要找引用
   - ROI：低。超出论文 scope，审稿人不会因为缺这个扣分

**建议**：只做 (a)——加 1-2 句升华 generalization insight。不展开 (b)(c)。

## 3. 实验分析挖掘：❌ 不值得做

现有分析已非常充分：
- 6 个模型的完整 dose-response 轨迹 + Algorithm 1 分类
- DiD（treated/untreated）排除 item-specific memorization
- Volume control 分解
- Cross-seed stability（3 seeds × 6 families）
- Cross-benchmark transfer（MMLU → ARC/GSM8K）
- Domain-level γ_d 异质性
- Detection signal failure modes
- 7 个 baseline 对比

**无明显遗漏 angle**。理论上可以做的（domain × dose 交互、per-subject 分析、item difficulty 分层）都需要新代码+新 IRT 运行，40h 内太冒险。

预计工时：4-8h（含代码 + 运行 + 验证 + 写入论文）。ROI ≈ 0，风险高。

## 4. Abstract/Intro hook：❌ 不值得做

当前 hook 已经有效：
- Abstract 第一句直接命中 gap："...widely assumed to inflate...yet its actual effect has never been systematically measured across model families" —— 清晰、准确、不过度
- Intro 第一段结尾的 punch line 很强："The implicit assumption is uniform inflation, but this has never been tested across model families"
- Contribution 列表结构清晰，3 条各有对应 section

**更激进的 hook**（如以 surprising finding 开头）可能更吸眼球，但也可能被审稿人认为 sensationalist。当前 framing 平衡了吸引力和学术严谨性。

预计工时：1-2h（改写 + 确保全文一致性）。ROI ≈ 0，风险中。

---

## 总结建议

| 维度 | 建议 | 工时 | ROI |
|------|------|------|-----|
| Related Work | 不动 | — | — |
| Discussion 深度 | 加 1-2 句 generalization insight | 15 min | 中 |
| 实验分析 | 不动 | — | — |
| Abstract/Intro | 不动 | — | — |

**40h 内最优操作清单**（总计 ~1h）：
1. 修复 results.tex L45 双 "demonstrating"（上轮终审 warning） — 15 min
2. 消除 2-3 处其他 trailing -ing 从句 — 15 min
3. 统一 MDES 四舍五入 — 5 min
4. Discussion 加 1 句 generalization insight 升华 — 15 min
5. 最终编译检查 — 10 min

**剩余 39h 用于**：排版微调、Appendix 校对、OpenReview submission form 填写、ARR Checklist 回答。不要做高风险改动。

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Controlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns---not uniform inflation. Two replicate across independent families (Benefit, Immune); two are Qwen2.5-specific and await replication. DiD finds no item-specific memorization (MDES 1.25--2.05pp); direction reproduces across seeds but magnitude is seed-sensitive.
4	
5	\paragraph{Contamination vs.\ additional training.}
6	SFT injection confounds contamination content, additional training volume ($+$33\% at d100), and LoRA parameter updates. A volume-matched control decomposes Falcon3's Benefit into ${\sim}$45\% quantity and ${\sim}$55\% content, though this single-family decomposition may not generalize. Non-Benefit patterns provide complementary evidence: non-benchmark MCQ data does not produce Collapse or model-specific divergence, so benchmark-specific content triggers qualitatively different responses.
7	
8	\paragraph{Implications for practice.}
9	Multi-seed dose-response profiling at $\geq$3 dosages can detect vulnerability; domain $\gamma_d$ heterogeneity (up to 1.8$\times$) motivates domain-level controls, and ContamCal can flag anomalous profiles given $J \geq 50$.
10	
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nControlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns---not uniform inflation. Two replicate across independent families (Benefit, Immune); two are Qwen2.5-specific and await replication. DiD finds no item-specific memorization (MDES 1.25--2.05pp); direction reproduces across seeds but magnitude is seed-sensitive.\n\n\\paragraph{Contamination vs.\\ additional training.}\nSFT injection confounds contamination content, additional training volume ($+$33\\% at d100), and LoRA parameter updates. A volume-matched control decomposes Falcon3's Benefit into ${\\sim}$45\\% quantity and ${\\sim}$55\\% content, though this single-family decomposition may not generalize. The most efficient approach is to compress within the conclusion itself since that's where the overflow is happening. Let me re-read the conclusion:
```

````text
```latex
\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}

Controlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns---not uniform inflation. 

Now I'm looking at the replication status and the confounding factors. Two effects replicate across independent families while two remain Qwen2.5-specific, and the DiD analysis shows no item-specific memorization despite seed sensitivity in magnitude. The core confound is that SFT injection simultaneously introduces contamination content, increases training volume by 33% at d100, and updates LoRA parameters---a volume-matched control on Falcon3's Benefit effect suggests roughly 45% comes from quantity and 55% from content, though this decomposition may not hold across other families.

The non-Benefit patterns are revealing: non-benchmark MCQ data doesn't trigger Collapse or model-specific divergence, indicating that benchmark-specific content drives the qualitatively different responses. Combined with the absence of item-specific memorization, this points to contamination operating through global capability modulation rather than lookup-table memorization---benchmark data functions as a domain-relevant training signal.

For practical implications, multi-seed dose-response profiling at three or more dosages can detect vulnerability, domain heterogeneity reaches up to 1.8× and warrants domain-level controls, and ContamCal can flag anomalous profiles with at least 50 items. I'm working through how to tighten this section about contamination mechanisms. The key insight is that non-benchmark data doesn't trigger the same divergence patterns, and combined with the lack of item-specific memorization, this points to contamination operating as a global capability shift rather than simple lookup—so I can consolidate these observations into a more streamlined argument. I'm working through the decomposition results more carefully—the volume-matched control breaks down Falcon3's benefit into roughly equal parts from quantity and content effects. Without evidence of item-level memorization, this points to contamination operating as a global capability shift rather than lookup-based learning. , so benchmark-specific content matters beyond volume. The null result on item-specific memorization combined with these patterns points to contamination working through global capability shifts rather than direct item lookup.

Now I'm calculating the character savings from these edits—removing the hedging clause about generalization, consolidating the complementary evidence framing, and tightening the final mechanism description should free up roughly 2.5 lines in the two-column format, which should be sufficient for the space constraints.
````

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Controlled SFT injection reveals a spectrum of dose-response patterns---not uniform inflation. Two replicate across independent families (Benefit, Immune); two are Qwen2.5-specific and await replication. DiD finds no item-specific memorization (MDES 1.25--2.05pp); direction reproduces across seeds but magnitude is seed-sensitive.
4	
5	\paragraph{Contamination vs.\ additional training.}
6	SFT injection confounds contamination content, additional training volume ($+$33\% at d100), and LoRA parameter updates. A volume-matched control decomposes Falcon3's Benefit into ${\sim}$45\% quantity and ${\sim}$55\% content, though this single-family decomposition may not generalize. Non-Benefit patterns provide complementary evidence: non-benchmark MCQ data does not produce Collapse or model-specific divergence, so benchmark-specific content triggers qualitatively different responses. Together with the absence of item-specific memorization (\S\ref{sec:memorization_test}), these results suggest contamination operates through global capability modulation---benchmark data functions as a domain-relevant training signal rather than a lookup table.
7	
8	\paragraph{Implications for practice.}
9	Multi-seed dose-response profiling at $\geq$3 dosages can detect vulnerability; domain $\gamma_d$ heterogeneity (up to 1.8$\times$) motivates domain-level controls, and ContamCal can flag anomalous profiles given $J \geq 50$.
10	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PtmXdbvZ6oB8gmjmwQmg1w`</summary>

```text
1	Benchmark contamination is widely assumed to inflate language model scores, yet its actual effect has never been systematically measured across model families. We perform controlled SFT injection at five dosage levels into six models (7B--14.7B) from five open-source families and discover a spectrum of dose-response patterns, not uniform inflation. Two patterns replicate across independent families: Immune (Phi-4, LLaMA-3.1) and Benefit (statistically significant for Gemma-2, BCa 95\% CI [$+$1.49, $+$3.14]\,pp; borderline for OLMo-2 and Falcon3). Collapse and V-recovery are each observed in one family (Qwen2.5) across three seeds but remain preliminary pending independent replication. Effects are benchmark-conditional: MMLU-targeted contamination improves GSM8K in five of six families, even those harmed on MMLU. Difference-in-differences analysis detects no item-specific memorization; a volume-matched control attributes 45\% of Benefit to data quantity. ContamCal, our diagnostic framework combining multi-signal exposure mapping with contamination-augmented IRT, enables domain-level contamination diagnosis with credible intervals---revealing that Social Sciences contamination effects are negative while STEM effects are positive, a pattern invisible to aggregate scoring. Out-of-sample validation on LLaMA-3.1-8B confirms that model family characteristics beyond baseline ability modulate vulnerability. Model-specific dose-response profiling is necessary for reliable benchmark interpretation.
2	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01CR9o3s2kMYcoP26HMfVfp3`</summary>

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	All experiments use models $\leq$14.7B parameters; 70B+ models may exhibit qualitatively different contamination dynamics. With $n\!=\!3$ seeds per family, effect \emph{direction} is reproducible but magnitude is seed-sensitive, and borderline classifications (e.g., OLMo-2) should be interpreted with caution.
5	
6	Our LoRA-based SFT injection differs from pretraining-stage leakage via web crawl ingestion, and dosage levels assume uniform injection of all benchmark items; real-world contamination may follow skewed exposure distributions. The AUGMENT protocol also increases training volume with dosage ($+$33\% at d100); a volume-matched control attributes ${\sim}$45\% of Benefit to data quantity, though this decomposition is available only for Falcon3.
7	
8	Out-of-sample validation covers only the Immune pattern; Collapse and V-recovery are Qwen2.5-specific and await independent-family replication. IRT is fitted on 300 subsampled items (2.1\% of MMLU), and RRF exposure scores are point estimates whose measurement uncertainty is not propagated into $\gamma$ credible intervals.
9	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NSxRTBeuYrZbiZcnxxsoGK`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Experimental Setup}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Controlled SFT Injection}
5	\label{sec:injection}
6	
7	We construct a controlled contamination testbed spanning five independent model families from five labs, trained on independent corpora with distinct pre-training recipes: Qwen2.5 \citep{qwen2024qwen25} (0.5B, 1.5B, 3B, 7B, 14B), Phi-4 \citep{abdin2024phi4} (14.7B), OLMo-2 \citep{olmo2team2025} (7B), Gemma-2 \citep{gemmateam2024gemma2} (9B), and Falcon3 \citep{tiiuae2024falcon3} (7B). All models are fine-tuned with a unified LoRA configuration \citep{hu2022lora} ($r=16$, $\alpha=32$, dropout$=0$, targeting all attention and MLP projections; lr$=5\times10^{-5}$, bf16, 1 epoch, batch size$=1$, gradient accumulation$=16$, warmup ratio$=0.03$; trainable parameters range from 0.45\% of Phi-4 14.7B to 2.10\% of Qwen2.5-0.5B) on a general-purpose SFT corpus into which benchmark items are injected at five dosage levels: d000 (0\%, clean control), d005 (5\%), d025 (25\%), d050 (50\%), and d100 (100\%). Contaminated items augment (not replace) the base SFT corpus, yielding 50.0k (d000), 50.8k (d005), 54.1k (d025), 58.3k (d050), and 66.5k (d100) total training examples ($+$33\% volume at d100 vs.\ d000).
8	
9	Qwen2.5 serves as the primary within-family series (five scale points, 0.5B--14B). Models at $\leq$3B are trained with two seeds at four dosage levels; 7B and 14B at all five dosages. The three cross-family models are each trained at five dosages with a single seed, serving as consistency checks rather than independent confirmatory analyses. This yields over 55 checkpoints spanning eight sizes across five families. We exclude 0.5B from MC IRT due to position bias (A-option rate: 46\% at d000 to 98\% at d100; \S\ref{sec:signal_failure}). We additionally validate our dose-response profiles on a held-out sixth family, LLaMA-3.1-8B \citep{grattafiori2024llama3} (Meta), which was not used during profile construction. LLaMA-3.1 is trained at three dosage levels (d000, d025, d100) to verify that Algorithm~\ref{alg:profile-classification} generalizes to unseen model families.
10	
11	\subsection{Benchmarks, Domains, and Evaluation}
12	\label{sec:benchmarks}
13	
14	We evaluate on three benchmarks spanning distinct cognitive demands and response formats.
15	
16	\textbf{MMLU} \citep{hendrycks2021mmlu} (5-shot): 14,042 multiple-choice items across 57 subjects, grouped into 8 cognitive domains (Math, Science, CS \& Engineering, Medical, Humanities, Social Sciences, Business \& Law, Other). This stratification reveals within-benchmark heterogeneity that aggregate scoring obscures (\S\ref{sec:results}).
17	
18	\textbf{ARC-Challenge} \citep{clark2018arc} (25-shot): 1,172 multiple-choice science reasoning items.
19	
20	\textbf{GSM8K} \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}: 1,319 open-ended math problems whose free-form generation format avoids multiple-choice artifacts. Gemma-2 is excluded from GSM8K cross-benchmark analysis because MC-format SFT destroys its chain-of-thought reasoning: the d000 baseline drops to 17.8\% (vs.\ 73--84\% for other families), indicating CoT collapse rather than a contamination effect.
21	
22	All multiple-choice evaluations use logprob-based scoring under an aligned instruction-template format with fixed few-shot counts; all 75 model--dosage configurations are evaluated under this identical protocol.
23	
24	We assess calibration quality via MAE (calibrated vs.\ ground-truth clean accuracy), Kendall $\tau$ (rank preservation), credible interval coverage, posterior predictive $p$-value (PPP), $\gamma$ direction ($P(\gamma\!<\!0 \mid \text{data})$), and split-$\hat{R}$ convergence.
25	
26	\subsection{Baselines}
27	\label{sec:baselines}
28	
29	We compare against seven baselines: \textbf{B0-Raw} (no adjustment), \textbf{B1-Remove\&Rescore} (drop flagged items; B1b uses RRF-filtered items), \textbf{B2-Single2PL+$\gamma$} (aggregate $\gamma$ without domain stratification), \textbf{B3-Vanilla2PL} ($\gamma=0$, isolating IRT ability estimation), \textbf{B4-LinearRegression} (residuals on exposure scores), \textbf{B5-Emperor} (mitigation strategies from \citet{sun2025emperor}; B5b uses the best-performing variant), and \textbf{B6-DCR} (our reimplementation following \citealp{xu2025dcr}).
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019c3qzTUme4LeW9SmukvmeB`</summary>

```text
140	\paragraph{Signal contribution to $\gamma$ estimation.}
141	Leave-one-out ablation on $\gamma$ recovery (Qwen2.5, $J=16$) confirms S4 (self-critique) as the dominant contributor: removing S4 incurs $4.9\times$ the $\gamma$ estimation loss of removing S2 (Min-K\%++). No signal is pure noise: the worst single-signal $\rho(E,\tau)$ is 0.072--0.118 (S2), still $5.5\times$ the random baseline. Marginal returns from three to two signals are negligible (dropping S2 leaves $\rho$ effectively unchanged), suggesting the current three-signal ensemble is near-optimal for the Qwen2.5 family.
142	
143	\paragraph{Full pipeline S2-removal test.}
144	To directly address whether S2 (Min-K\%++) harms downstream calibration, we refit the full IRT pipeline excluding S2 from the RRF fusion. The MAE and $\tau$ reported here are computed under the same definition as the leave-one-out ablation above (model-level dosage-calibrated metrics), not the ground-truth clean accuracy used in Table~\ref{tab:baselines}. Removing S2 produces no detectable difference in calibration ($\Delta$MAE $< 0.001$) or model fit ($\Delta$PPP $= 0.01$) on this test population. However, the Qwen2.5 1.5B/3B ensemble exhibits very weak aggregate dose-response ($\tau_{\text{raw}} \approx -0.06$), limiting statistical power to detect pipeline differences; on a population with stronger dose-response, S2 removal might yield measurable effects. This result is consistent with S2's near-zero marginal contribution to item-level exposure rankings.
145	
146	\section{Single-Signal Pipeline Comparison}
147	\label{sec:s1_only_pipeline}
148	
149	To evaluate whether multi-signal fusion is necessary for downstream IRT estimation, we compare the full RRF pipeline against an end-to-end single-signal alternative using Min-K\%++ (S1-only). Because the two pipelines differ in both exposure scoring and item selection (RRF uses a random 300-item subsample; S1-only uses the top 300 items by Min-K\%++ score), this is an end-to-end pipeline comparison rather than a controlled single-variable ablation.
```

```text
382	\section{Post-hoc Power Analysis (MDES)}
383	\label{sec:mdes}
384	
385	Table~\ref{tab:mdes_mmlu} and Table~\ref{tab:mdes_arc} report the minimum detectable effect size (MDES) for the treated vs.\ untreated DiD comparison at d025 (80\% power, $\alpha=0.05$, two-sided). MDES is computed as the effect size detectable given each model's observed variance, the treated/untreated split sizes, and a two-sample $t$-test power calculation. At d100, all benchmark items are treated ($n_{\text{untreated}}=0$), precluding DiD analysis; d025 is reported as the primary dosage level.
386	
387	\begin{table}[h]
388	\centering
389	\caption{Post-hoc MDES for MMLU DiD ($n_{\text{treated}}=3{,}540$, $n_{\text{untreated}}=10{,}502$).}
```
